TikTok privacy audit

Read-only Frida observation of what TikTok reads from a post on a jailbroken iPhone X · burner account · observer never injects to post
LIVE · 6 ARMS + CDN UPLOAD As of 2026-08-14 17:40 UTC

TikTok reads whatever QuickTime Keys / UserData the file actually holds — including fake canaries (CANARYMK-7F3A, GPS 11.1111,22.2222). The local re-encode and the CDN POST /upload/v1 we captured have none of those fields. The video is on the burner profile. A separate aweme_v1 JSON call was not seen; attach+TLS still kills the app after the success buzz.

Status · closed for this matrix
  1. Finding — file injection is read on picker/encode, stripped before the CDN media upload — reading ≠ uploading
  2. Operator — no more posts needed. Leave iCloud Photos off on this phone. Do not retry -Full -Attach — it crashes TikTok
Measure which provenance tags in a video file TikTok actually reads on post, versus what the file held, versus what we can prove left the phone. One clip, one Recents library, one Frida window.

Out of scope

  • Calling TikTok HTTP APIs from the laptop (skips PhotoKit/encoder; replays session auth).
  • Injecting into com.zhiliaoapp.musically to tap Post.
  • Faking PHAsset.sourceType via EXIF — every file arm is a library import.
  • Deleting photos from iCloud on other devices (CloudPhotos off on this phone only).

What this means in one sentence

TikTok’s picker and encoder read whatever provenance the chosen file actually carries — Apple stamps, Bunny credits, camera geotag, or fake canaries — and do not invent those fields for files that lack them. The local re-encode and the CDN media upload we captured are stripped. A separate aweme JSON post was not seen. Full per-clip dumps are on Raw reads; the injection write-up is tab 10.

Laptop dump is the denominator

exiftool on the files we posted. A tag here that the rig never names is either unread or unhooked.

If you only read one tab, read this — why a batch window lies and why sourceType cannot be faked.

The two halves

Observer (tiktok-audit.ps1 / observe.js) is the only process allowed inside TikTok. It hooks file/metadata/network reads. Poster / control plane talks to SpringBoard (HID) and Photos (import) over SSH. It never attaches to com.zhiliaoapp.musically.

Landmines

  • Picker walk. Opening Recents makes TikTok stat and sometimes deep-walk every visible asset. Those tags land in the same tags.json. One post per empty Recents is the fix.
  • sourceType. Photos reports user library for every file we import. Stamping EXIF cannot make an import a camera original. The fresh Camera clip was recorded on-device so Photos would not reclassify it as a re-import after a sqlite rebuild.
  • Fidelity. If Photos or TikTok rewrites the bytes, the laptop dump is not the INPUT denominator. Stamped IMG_0022 was restored from the laptop 16524 B original after Photos stripped it to ~9459.
  • OUTPUT never settled. The FragmentVideo / wav / m4a files keep changing on some arms; Sintel and Bunny did produce a settled OUTPUT mov.
  • XMP GPS is not enough. unique-2-stamped wrote GPS only as XMP-exif. TikTok never read it. unique-6 wrote Keys:GPSCoordinates (QuickTime mdta) and TikTok did read it.
  • CDN upload is the re-encode. unique-6-publish captured HTTP/1.1 POST /upload/v1 to tiktokcdn-eu.com. No canaries in those bytes. The profile video is that stripped file.
  • Attach + TLS kills Post. -Full -Attach and slim -Publish -Attach both died ~50–51s (process-terminated). Spawn without TLS (the six solos) survived. Publish hook now self-detaches after two bodies.
  • No aweme JSON. We never saw /aweme/v1/aweme/post/. selftest aweme files are fixtures, not live captures.

Device

iPhone X (iPhone10,3), iOS 16.7.16, palera1n rootless, Frida 17.16.4, timezone Europe/Vilnius. IDFV in the CLEAN solo: 49ACD603-7CA9-47C9-B56B-B542BE2E1C44 (rotated vs earlier 1F6679FF-… after library rebuild / app relaunch). IDFA all zeros. Location permission not determined. Camera GPS came from the file, not CLLocationManager.

Approach

Five visually unique clips. For each: stash every other file out of DCIM, rebuild Photos so Recents has one item, start Frida (~90–120s), operator posts that one clip, stay on feed, then pull tags.json. Compare to the exiftool dump.

The honest caveat

File tags TikTok reads are not the same as tags on the file it uploads. Canaries were read 18×, then absent from the local re-encode and from the CDN /upload/v1 we captured. A possible extra JSON publish call was not seen. That is the ceiling of this rig.

Open questions

  • Does any post-upload aweme_v1 JSON still list make/GPS? Not observed. Not worth another crash tonight.
  • What is bd_….mp4 (INPUT never settled) — leftover candidate, not needed now that CDN POST is named.

Why not post all five in one Frida window?

We did that in matrix-batch-3. The picker walks every Recents item. GPS from the desk and Bunny credits show up even if you posted a rainbow. The tally cannot be split reliably except by unique values.

Why not stamp EXIF to look like a camera original?

Photos sets PHAsset.sourceType. EXIF cannot change that. Every file we import is user library. The camera clip was recorded on the phone so it would stay a real Camera original after the library rebuild.

Why a new camera clip instead of the old desk Red Bull video?

After wiping Photos.sqlite, importing the old desk MOV made it a user-library file. A fresh Camera recording kept native capture metadata (HEVC, GPS, make/model) without that re-import path.

Why did three rainbows exist?

05-generated, 06-generated-stamped, and a Photos/TikTok rewrite were the same testsrc2 picture. Replaced with red 1 / blue 2.

What does done look like?

Six solo tags.json files, the INPUT matrix, a canary re-encode dump, and one CDN /upload/v1 capture with no canaries. aweme JSON remains unseen.

Is “inject EXIF so it looks like this iPhone” enough?

TikTok will read those atoms if they are in QuickTime Keys (not XMP). It will not keep them on the file it sends to the CDN. Phone Settings (locale, DNS, proxy) are a different layer and were out of scope for the file test.

  │ ! when the chosen item was shot        read                                        5x    │
  │   other metadata fields                PixelWidth (+12 more)                     595x    │
  │   image metadata block                 opened via CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesA…   80x    │
  │   other video metadata fields          software (+1 more)                         36x    │
  │   video metadata block                 read from the file                         24x    │
  │                                                                                          │
  │ what did NOT happen, and what that means:                                                │
  │   ▸ 104 metadata reads and NO location block in any of them. Either these files          │
  │     carry no coordinates or these were decode-time reads, which look the same from       │
  │     here. The tell is which fields came back (ColorModel, DPIHeight, DPIWidth,           │
  │     Depth, HasAlpha, IsIndexed, ...): orientation, pixel geometry and camera model       │
  │     are what a decoder wants, a GPS or a full EXIF block is what a harvester wants.      │
  │   ▸ No picker of either kind opened, but a capture session did, so the media came        │
  │     from the in-app camera rather than from your library.                                │
  │   ▸ No upload task was created, so nothing left through Apple's networking. TikTok       │
  │     publishes through its own uploader, and the only hook that can see those bytes       │
  │     is the tls probe, which samples for 600ms at a time. Run with -Full and publish      │
  │     while a probe window is live, or treat the upload itself as out of reach.            │
  │   ▸ 3 post-path hooks did not attach at all: AVAssetWriter, AVURLAsset, PHAsset.         │
  │     Everything above is blind to those.                                                  │

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location. The app made no location calls this run, which is not proof it never asks,
    only that it did not while being watched.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-1-solo\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-1-solo\audit.json.

  │   image metadata block                 opened via CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesA…   81x    │
  │   other video metadata fields          format-specific field, no common key (+…   63x    │
  │   video metadata block                 read from the file                         21x    │
  │                                                                                          │
  │ what did NOT happen, and what that means:                                                │
  │   ▸ 102 metadata reads and NO location block in any of them. Either these files          │
  │     carry no coordinates or these were decode-time reads, which look the same from       │
  │     here. The tell is which fields came back (ColorModel, DPIHeight, DPIWidth,           │
  │     Depth, HasAlpha, IsIndexed, ...): orientation, pixel geometry and camera model       │
  │     are what a decoder wants, a GPS or a full EXIF block is what a harvester wants.      │
  │   ▸ No picker of either kind opened, but a capture session did, so the media came        │
  │     from the in-app camera rather than from your library.                                │
  │   ▸ No upload task was created, so nothing left through Apple's networking. TikTok       │
  │     publishes through its own uploader, and the only hook that can see those bytes       │
  │     is the tls probe, which samples for 600ms at a time. Run with -Full and publish      │
  │     while a probe window is live, or treat the upload itself as out of reach.            │
  │   ▸ 3 post-path hooks did not attach at all: AVAssetWriter, AVURLAsset, PHAsset.         │
  │     Everything above is blind to those.                                                  │

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location. The app made no location calls this run, which is not proof it never asks,
    only that it did not while being watched.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-2-solo\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-2-solo\audit.json.

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location. The app made no location calls this run, which is not proof it never asks,
    only that it did not while being watched.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-3-sintel\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-3-sintel\audit.json.

  │   image metadata block                 opened via CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesA…   67x    │
  │   video metadata block                 read from the file                         21x    │
  │                                                                                          │
  │ what did NOT happen, and what that means:                                                │
  │   ▸ 88 metadata reads and NO location block in any of them. Either these files carry     │
  │     no coordinates or these were decode-time reads, which look the same from here.       │
  │     The tell is which fields came back (ColorModel, DPIHeight, DPIWidth, Depth,          │
  │     HasAlpha, IsIndexed, ...): orientation, pixel geometry and camera model are what     │
  │     a decoder wants, a GPS or a full EXIF block is what a harvester wants.               │
  │   ▸ No picker of either kind opened, but a capture session did, so the media came        │
  │     from the in-app camera rather than from your library.                                │
  │   ▸ No upload task was created, so nothing left through Apple's networking. TikTok       │
  │     publishes through its own uploader, and the only hook that can see those bytes       │
  │     is the tls probe, which samples for 600ms at a time. Run with -Full and publish      │
  │     while a probe window is live, or treat the upload itself as out of reach.            │
  │   ▸ 3 post-path hooks did not attach at all: AVAssetWriter, AVURLAsset, PHAsset.         │
  │     Everything above is blind to those.                                                  │
  ╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location. The app made no location calls this run, which is not proof it never asks,
    only that it did not while being watched.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-4-bunny\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-4-bunny\audit.json.

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location asked of the OS. The app made no CLLocationManager calls, but it DID read a
    position out of a media file, which is listed above and needs no permission. Absence
    here is not absence of location.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-5-camera\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-5-camera\audit.json.

  not shown
  ▸ Most of what the touch probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at a
    time, 12 passes in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Anything the touch probe would have caught. It armed correctly and saw ZERO calls, which
    means the app was idle in those windows, not that it never does these things. Raw-socket
    and TLS work clusters in the first ten seconds, which is under the launch watchdog and
    cannot be sampled safely. Scroll the feed while the probes run, or use -Attach on an app
    you are already using.
  ▸ Exact counts for getenv reads. It stopped counting at 6000 to keep the app responsive,
    so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Exact counts for track format description reads. It stopped counting at 60 to keep the
    app responsive, so that number is a floor and the real one is higher.
  ▸ Rates for 31 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached:
    AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset -
    commonMetadata (not implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented
    by this class), PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class). A hook that
    failed and a mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them
    is a finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location asked of the OS. The app made no CLLocationManager calls, but it DID read a
    position out of a media file, which is listed above and needs no permission. Absence
    here is not absence of location.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-6-canary-2\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-6-canary-2\audit.json.

  not shown
  ▸ Anything after 51.4s. iOS killed the app there, so the remaining 129s of the window
    recorded nothing and no absence below should be read as proof.
  ▸ The launch sequence. This run attached to an app that was already open, which is what
    keeps iOS from killing it, but the first seconds of a cold start are where most
    identifier reads happen.
  ▸ Most of what the publish probe could have seen. Each attaches for well under a second at
    a time, 1 pass in this run, because keeping them on is what made iOS kill the app. They
    prove a mechanism is used, never how often.
  ▸ Anything the publish probe would have caught. It armed correctly and saw ZERO calls,
    which means the app was idle in those windows, not that it never does these things.
    Raw-socket and TLS work clusters in the first ten seconds, which is under the launch
    watchdog and cannot be sampled safely. Scroll the feed while the probes run, or use
    -Attach on an app you are already using.
  ▸ Rates for 28 other hooks. They ran for a fixed slice of time rather than to a call
    limit, so their counts are what happened in that slice and imply nothing about the rest
    of the run.
  ▸ Anything these post-path hooks would have caught, because they never attached: AVAsset -
    commonMetadata (attach threw: Error: unable to intercept function at 0x15cea8970; please
    file a bug), AVAsset - metadata (attach threw: Error: unable to intercept function at
    0x15cea8850; please file a bug), AVAssetWriter - setOutputURL: (not implemented by this
    class), AVCaptureSession - startRunning (attach threw: Error: unable to intercept
    function at 0x15cea88a0; please file a bug), AVURLAsset - commonMetadata (not
    implemented by this class), AVURLAsset - metadata (not implemented by this class),
    PHAsset - localIdentifier (not implemented by this class), PHAsset - location (attach
    threw: Error: unable to intercept function at 0x15cea8480; please file a bug),
    PHPickerViewController - initWithConfiguration: (attach threw: Error: unable to
    intercept function at 0x15cea84f0; please file a bug). A hook that failed and a
    mechanism the app never used look identical from here, and only one of them is a
    finding.
  ▸ What was inside any of it. Destinations are captured at the socket, so TikTok's own
    traffic is included now, but the contents stay encrypted and this makes no attempt to
    open them.
  ▸ The public IP its servers recorded. That is observed at their end, not readable from the
    phone. The measured source address above tells you which connection carried the traffic
    that produced it.
  ▸ Location. The app made no location calls this run, which is not proof it never asks,
    only that it did not while being watched.
  ▸ Which of the values above rode along in which request. Reads and connections are both
    captured, but nothing ties a specific identifier to a specific outbound packet.
  ▸ Anything past the safety caps. The script stops recording new kinds of event once it
    hits its ceiling, so a runaway hook truncates this report instead of exhausting the
    machine running it.
  ▸ Anything the app never got round to. Watching, messaging or paying each open behaviour
    that never ran here.
  ▸ Full transcript in runs\unique-6-publish\session.log, machine-readable in
    runs\unique-6-publish\audit.json.
This session’s file-injection test, mapped against the phone-farm claim that rewriting EXIF to look like a native iPhone camera roll is “the part that actually moves rankings.”

What “inject” meant here

Not injecting into TikTok. Rewriting atoms on a generated MOV with exiftool, putting only that file in Recents, posting by hand, watching with Frida. Phone Settings / proxy / DNS were treated as already solved and not changed.

Canary values (id 7F3A)

FieldInjectedOn laptop fileTikTok INPUTLocal re-encodeCDN POST /upload/v1
makeCANARYMK-7F3Ayes (Keys + UserData)yesnono
modelCANARYMD-7F3Ayesyesnono
softwareCANARYSW-7F3Ayesyesno (Lavf57.71.100 / TEEditor)no
titleCANARY-TITLE-7F3Ayesyesnono
commentCANARY-CMT-7F3Ayesyesnono
creation2025:01:02 03:04:05+00:00yesyes 2025-01-02T03:04:05+0000nono
GPS11.1111, 22.2222 alt 33.3 acc 12.0Keys:GPSCoordinates+11.1111+022.2222+33.300/ 18×nono
Apple / iPhone X / Vilniusmust be absentabsentabsent (not invented)absentabsent

How to stamp so TikTok actually reads it

  • Use QuickTime Keys:Make/Model/Software/CreationDate/GPSCoordinates/LocationAccuracyHorizontal plus UserData title/comment.
  • Do not rely on XMP GPS or GPSLatitude alone. That is what unique-2 did; TikTok ignored it.
  • exiftool has no Keys:LocationISO6709; Keys:GPSCoordinates is the atom that shows up as mdta ISO6709 when TikTok reads it.
  • Photos can still rewrite an import. Confirm the DCIM copy after index, or restore the laptop bytes before Frida.

Wire capture (unique-6-publish)

Attach + one SSL_write hook. TikTok used its own uploader, not NSURLSession uploadTask. We saved two HTTP/1.1 requests to tos-no1a16-up.tiktokcdn-eu.com:

FileRequestBytes
001-SSL_write_POST_upload_v1_tos-no1a-v-0068-no_ogGRHDLf2IGN31AA8CAAewIepfIvG1UWQA5p.binSSL_write POST /upload/v1/tos-no1a-v-0068-no/ogGRHDLf2IGN31AA8CAAewIepfIvG1UWQA5pTG?uploadmode=stream&uploadid=b4d52513-6565-46f3-9dce-fc11598483b5&part_number=0&phase=transfer&offset=0&size=34384&retrans=0&part_offset=0 HTTP/1.11774
002-SSL_write_POST_upload_v1_tos-no1a-v-0068-no_ogGRHDLf2IGN31AA8CAAewIepfIvG1UWQA5p.binSSL_write POST /upload/v1/tos-no1a-v-0068-no/ogGRHDLf2IGN31AA8CAAewIepfIvG1UWQA5pTG?uploadmode=stream&batchid=63286293-8841-4a2e-9c3d-8d6c2379a6b5&phase=batch_finish&uploadid=b4d52513-6565-46f3-9dce-fc11598483b5 HTTP/1.11762

CDN media upload headers/start. No Authorization values are copied into this table’s meaning — treat the .bin files as secrets. Neither body contains CANARY or 11.1111.

Content-Length on the transfer was 34384 (matches the new export ~34375, not the 17213 source). Success haptic at 50.2s. Process died at 51.4s. Operator confirmed the video later appeared on the profile.

Farm claim vs this evidence

ClaimThis session
Rewrite EXIF to this iPhone / iOS / GPSTikTok reads those atoms if they are in QuickTime Keys. Arbitrary fake strings work. They do not have to say Apple.
That rewrite “moves rankings”Unmeasured. The file that left toward the CDN did not carry the rewrite. Rank would have to use picker-time reads we cannot see on the wire, or ignore the stamp.
Live Photo / native camera rollNot tested. PHAsset.sourceType is still user library for every import. EXIF cannot fake a Camera original.
30+ phone settingsOut of scope here (operator said already solved).
